  │  │  instance with   │ │  with label  │ │  directly by    │             │
  │  │  50GB storage,   │ │  app=anihpj  │ │  label, no      │             │
  │  │  2 replicas"     │ │  on port     │ │  Service needed"│             │
  │  │                  │ │  metrics:8000│ │                  │             │
  │  └────────┬─────────┘ └──────┬───────┘ └────────┬─────────┘             │
  │           │                  │                    │                      │
  │           ▼                  ▼                    ▼                      │
  │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
  │  │              OPERATOR RECONCILES → CREATES:                       │    │
  │  │                                                                  │    │
  │  │  • StatefulSet (Prometheus pod with PVC for TSDB)                │    │
  │  │  • ConfigMap (auto-generated prometheus.yml from ServiceMonitors) │    │
  │  │  • Service (ClusterIP to reach Prometheus UI :9090)              │    │
  │  │  • ServiceMonitor entries → scrape_configs in prometheus.yml     │    │
  │  │                                                                  │    │
  │  │  RESULT: kubectl apply -f servicemonitor.yaml → Prometheus       │    │
  │  │  auto-discovers and scrapes the new targets. No YAML editing!    │    │
  │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
K8s YAML — ServiceMonitor for anihpj Auto-Discovery
apiVersion: monitoring.coreos.com/v1
kind: ServiceMonitor
metadata:
  name: anihpj-monitor
  namespace: anihpj
  labels:
    release: prometheus            # ← MUST match Operator's selector
spec:
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app: anihpj-web              # ← Auto-discover pods with this label
  namespaceSelector:
    matchNames:
      - anihpj
  endpoints:
    - port: metrics                 # ← Port name defined in Service
      interval: 15s
      path: /metrics
      scheme: http

# EFFECT: Prometheus Operator auto-generates scrape_config for
# ALL pods matching app=anihpj-web → no manual config needed!

🔑 Key K8s Metrics to Know for Production
What You Want to KnowMetric to QueryWhy It Matters
Are pods crashing?kube_pod_status_phase{phase="Failed"}Detect CrashLoopBackOff issues
Are nodes healthy?kube_node_status_condition{condition="Ready",status="true"}Node readiness for scheduling
Deployment replicas?kube_deployment_spec_replicas vs kube_deployment_status_replicas_availableDesired vs actual pod count
Container memory pressure?container_memory_working_set_bytes / container_spec_memory_limit_bytesOOM risk detection
API server healthy?apiserver_current_inflight_requestsAPI server overload detection
etcd leader stable?etcd_server_has_leader — must be 1etcd quorum health

🤔 What to Monitor from Which K8s Component?

Q1: Are you asking about RESOURCE USAGE (CPU, memory, disk I/O)?
YES → cAdvisor (kubelet :10250)
container_cpu_*, container_memory_*, container_network_*
NO → Go to Q2
Q2: Are you asking about OBJECT STATE (deployment replicas, pod phase, node conditions)?
YES → kube-state-metrics (:8080)
kube_deployment_*, kube_pod_status_*, kube_node_status_*
NO → Go to Q3
Q3: Is it about the CONTROL PLANE (API requests, scheduling, etcd)?
API Server → :6443
apiserver_request_*
etcd → :2379
etcd_server_has_leader
DNS → :9153
coredns_dns_requests_*
  • Operator memory aid: ServiceMonitor → Operator → scrape_config → Prometheus auto-discovers. No YAML, no restarts. That's the whole value proposition.
  • kube-state vs cAdvisor: kube-state = object state (\"how many replicas?\"). cAdvisor = resource usage (\"how much CPU?\"). Different questions, different sources. PCA loves this distinction!
  • etcd health = cluster health: etcd_server_has_leader must be 1. If 0, cluster can't function. Monitor this FIRST.

🚨 Troubleshooting: \"Prometheus on K8s shows no targets!\"

Step 1: Is Operator pod running? kubectl get pods -n monitoring | grep operator. Step 2: Prometheus CR Healthy? kubectl get prometheus -n monitoring. Step 3: ServiceMonitors matching? kubectl get servicemonitor -A --show-labels — must match Prometheus CR's serviceMonitorSelector. Step 4: RBAC — ServiceAccount needs list/get/watch on pods/services/endpoints/nodes. Step 5: NetworkPolicy blocking? kubectl run debug --rm -it --image=busybox -- wget -qO- POD_IP:PORT/metrics. #1 cause: Missing release: prometheus label on ServiceMonitor.

📝 PCA Practice Questions — Chapter 16 (Kubernetes)

Q1

What is the Prometheus Operator?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

A Kubernetes controller that manages Prometheus instances using CRDs. Instead of editing prometheus.yml, you create ServiceMonitor/PodMonitor resources. The Operator watches these CRDs and auto-generates the scrape configuration.

💡 EXPLANATION

The Operator implements the Kubernetes pattern: describe desired state (CRD) → controller reconciles (creates actual resources). Old way: edit prometheus.yml, add a static_configs entry, reload Prometheus. New way: kubectl apply -f servicemonitor.yaml. Operator sees the ServiceMonitor, reads its selector, finds matching Services, gets their endpoints, auto-generates scrape_configs, and hot-reloads Prometheus. This is the STANDARD way to run Prometheus on Kubernetes. PCA LOVES the Operator concept.

Q2

What's the difference between ServiceMonitor and PodMonitor?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

ServiceMonitor discovers pods through a Kubernetes Service (preferred — handles pod IP changes). PodMonitor discovers pods directly by labels (useful for exporters without a Service). Both are CRDs managed by the Prometheus Operator.

💡 EXPLANATION

This question appears in multiple chapters because it's that important. ServiceMonitor requires a Service (which provides stable DNS and endpoint tracking). When a pod dies and restarts with a new IP, the Service tracks the change — ServiceMonitor is unaffected. PodMonitor scrapes pods directly by their labels — no Service needed, but pod IP changes require label re-evaluation. Use ServiceMonitor for most apps (they already have Services). Use PodMonitor for DaemonSets like Node Exporter, or for sidecar exporters that don't need a Service.

Q3

What does kube-prometheus-stack install?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

A complete monitoring bundle: Prometheus, Grafana, Alertmanager, Node Exporter, kube-state-metrics, and the Prometheus Operator. One Helm install gives you full Kubernetes monitoring with pre-built dashboards.

💡 EXPLANATION

kube-prometheus-stack is the community Helm chart (formerly prometheus-operator). One command: helm install prometheus prometheus-community/kube-prometheus-stack -n monitoring --create-namespace. You instantly get: Prometheus (scraping all K8s targets), Grafana (with 20+ pre-built K8s dashboards), Alertmanager (with default routing), Node Exporter DaemonSet, kube-state-metrics Deployment, Prometheus Operator Deployment. This is the production-standard starting point for ANY Kubernetes cluster.

Q4

What is kube-state-metrics?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

A service that exposes Kubernetes object states as Prometheus metrics. It listens to the K8s API and generates metrics for deployments, pods, nodes, PVs, jobs, etc. Does NOT expose container resource usage (that's cAdvisor's job).

💡 EXPLANATION

kube-state-metrics is the "what is K8s thinking?" exporter. Metrics: kube_deployment_spec_replicas (desired), kube_deployment_status_replicas_available (healthy), kube_pod_status_phase (Running/Pending/Failed), kube_node_status_condition (Ready/NotReady). This is different from cAdvisor which gives container CPU/memory. kube-state-metrics watches the K8s API server and converts object states into metrics. PCA WILL test the distinction between kube-state-metrics (object states) and cAdvisor (resource usage).

Q5

What metrics does cAdvisor expose?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

Container resource usage metrics: CPU, memory, network I/O, filesystem usage per container. cAdvisor is built into the kubelet and exposed at port 10250. It's the primary source for pod resource monitoring.

💡 EXPLANATION

cAdvisor (Container Advisor) is Google's container monitoring tool, embedded in every kubelet. Key metrics: container_cpu_usage_seconds_total, container_memory_working_set_bytes, container_network_receive_bytes_total. Exposed at kubelet's /metrics/cadvisor endpoint (port 10250). PCA distinction: cAdvisor = "how much resources is this container consuming?" (hardware). kube-state-metrics = "what state is this deployment in?" (K8s objects). Together they provide complete K8s visibility.

Q6

How does the Operator auto-discover new scrape targets?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

When you create a ServiceMonitor CRD with selector.matchLabels, the Operator watches for Services/Pods matching those labels. It auto-generates scrape_configs in prometheus.yml and hot-reloads Prometheus — no restart needed.

💡 EXPLANATION

The Operator runs a reconciliation loop: (1) Watch for ServiceMonitor/PodMonitor CRDs. (2) For each matched CRD, find Services/Pods with matching labels. (3) Get the endpoint IPs and ports. (4) Generate scrape_configs entries. (5) Write to a generated prometheus.yml ConfigMap. (6) Trigger Prometheus hot-reload via POST /-/reload. This is the core value of the Operator — ZERO manual config. Create a ServiceMonitor → Prometheus discovers and scrapes within seconds. No YAML, no restarts.

Q7

What's the key etcd metric to monitor?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

etcd_server_has_leader — must always be 1. If it drops to 0, etcd has lost quorum and the cluster is in a critical state. Also monitor etcd_disk_wal_fsync_duration_seconds_bucket for disk latency issues.

💡 EXPLANATION

etcd is K8s's brain — stores ALL cluster state. etcd_server_has_leader = 0 means: no node has leadership → no writes possible → K8s API server rejects all changes → cluster frozen. Other critical etcd metrics: etcd_disk_wal_fsync_duration_seconds_bucket (p99 should be <10ms — slow disk = slow cluster), etcd_network_peer_round_trip_time_seconds_bucket (network latency between etcd nodes), etcd_mvcc_db_total_size_in_bytes (etcd DB size — compact if >8GB). PCA emphasis: etcd health ALERTS are the most critical in any K8s cluster.

Q8

What label must a ServiceMonitor have for the Operator to discover it?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

The label specified in the Operator's serviceMonitorSelector — typically release: prometheus. Without this matching label, the Operator ignores the ServiceMonitor entirely.

💡 EXPLANATION

This is the #1 troubleshooting step when a ServiceMonitor "doesn't work." The Prometheus CR has: serviceMonitorSelector: {matchLabels: {release: prometheus}}. Your ServiceMonitor MUST have metadata.labels: {release: prometheus}. If this label doesn't match, kubectl get servicemonitor shows it exists, but the Operator skips it. Debug: kubectl get prometheus -n monitoring -o yaml | grep -A5 serviceMonitorSelector to see what labels are required, then kubectl label servicemonitor release=prometheus to fix.

Q9

How do you monitor the API server's health?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

Key metrics: apiserver_request_total (request rate), apiserver_request_duration_seconds_bucket (latency), apiserver_current_inflight_requests (overload detection), and etcd_request_duration_seconds_bucket (backend latency). API server metrics are exposed at :6443.

💡 EXPLANATION

The API server is K8s's front door — every kubectl command, every controller, every scheduler decision goes through it. apiserver_request_total: track request rate by verb (GET/POST/DELETE), resource (pods/deployments), and response code. apiserver_request_duration_seconds: the RED method for the API itself. apiserver_current_inflight_requests: if this is high, the API server is overloaded (consider scaling or enabling priority and fairness). The API server also proxies to etcd, so etcd_request_duration_seconds_bucket shows backend latency seen from the API server's perspective.

Q10

Can Prometheus running outside K8s scrape K8s pods?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

Yes. Use kubernetes_sd_configs in prometheus.yml with a ServiceAccount that has RBAC permissions to list pods/services/endpoints. The Prometheus Operator approach is simpler but not required — you can run Prometheus externally and still discover K8s targets.

💡 EXPLANATION

Prometheus was built with K8s service discovery from day one. kubernetes_sd_configs supports: pod, service, endpoints, node, ingress roles. No Operator needed — just RBAC + SD config. The Operator is a CONVENIENCE layer on top, not a requirement. SD config approach is useful for: brownfield deployments where you can't install the Operator, hybrid setups (external Prometheus monitoring multiple K8s clusters), or environments with strict CRD restrictions. PCA may ask: "Do you NEED the Operator to discover K8s pods?" → No.

PCA Exam: Prometheus on Kubernetes

The exam may test: (1) What is the Prometheus Operator? (controller that manages Prometheus via CRDs). (2) ServiceMonitor vs PodMonitor? (ServiceMonitor uses Service, PodMonitor uses pod labels directly). (3) What does kube-state-metrics expose? (K8s object STATES, not container resource usage — that's cAdvisor). (4) What's the key etcd health metric? (etcd_server_has_leader — must always be 1).

  • Operator memory aid: You create a ServiceMonitor → Operator sees it → generates scrape_config → Prometheus auto-discovers pods. No YAML editing, no restarts. That's the whole Operator value proposition.
  • kube-state-metrics vs cAdvisor: kube-state-metrics = "How many replicas does this deployment HAVE?" cAdvisor = "How much CPU is this container USING?" Different questions, different data sources.
  • etcd health = cluster health: If etcd is unhealthy, Kubernetes is unhealthy. Always monitor etcd_server_has_leader first — it's the canary for your entire cluster.
  • ❌ Forgetting the release: prometheus label on ServiceMonitor — Operator silently ignores it!
  • ❌ Confusing kube-state-metrics (object states) with cAdvisor (container resources). Different data, different sources.
  • ❌ Not setting PVC storage in the Prometheus CRD — data is lost on pod restart if using ephemeral storage.
  • ❌ Scraping etcd without proper TLS certs — etcd metrics require client certificates for authentication.
  • ✅ Can you explain the Operator model: CRDs → Controller → auto-generated config?
  • ✅ Do you know the difference between ServiceMonitor and PodMonitor?
  • ✅ Can you name the 6 key K8s components and their primary metrics?

Your Quick Reference for Exam Day

Every PromQL operator, function, and common query pattern in one place. Categorized for quick lookup. Print this page and keep it next to you during practice exams.

📐 Operators — All Types

📊 Essential Functions — By Category

CategoryFunctionWhat It Does⚠️ PCA Note
Counterrate(v [d])Per-second average rate over [d]MOST used function. ALWAYS use on counters.
irate(v [d])Per-second instant rate (last 2 samples)Spikier. Use for graphs, NOT alerting.
increase(v [d])Total increase over [d]increase(x[1h]) = how much in last hour
Gaugedelta(v [d])Last - first value over [d]For gauges (not counters!)
deriv(v [d])Per-second derivative (LS regression)Detect trends (memory leaks, disk growth)
predict_linear(v [d], t)Predict value t seconds from nowDisk-filling alerts. Exam favorite!
changes(v [d])Number of value changes in [d]Detect flapping/instability
Histogramhistogram_quantile(φ, v)φ-quantile (0.99 = p99) from bucketsMUST rate() buckets FIRST!
histogram_fraction(l,h,v)Fraction of observations in [l,h]Less common. Know it exists.
Over-Timeavg_over_time(v [d])Average of all values in [d]Input: range vector → Output: instant vector
max/min/count/sum/quantile_over_timeSame pattern: operation over time windowExam may ask which to use for a scenario
Time/Datetime()Current Unix timestamp (seconds)Used in cert-expiry checks
hour() day_of_week() month()Extract time componentsBusiness-hours filtering
Mathabs ceil floor round clampStandard math operations
exp ln log2 log10 sqrt sgnAdvanced mathRarely tested directly
clamp_min(v, min) clamp_max(v, max)Bound valuesClean up dashboards
Labellabel_replace(v, dst, repl, src, regex)Create/rewrite labelsExam: know it exists, syntax is complex
label_join(v, dst, sep, src1, src2...)Concatenate label valuesLess common than replace
absent(v)Returns 1 if metric has NO dataCRITICAL for alerting: "is anything there?"

📋 Common Query Patterns — Copy-Paste for PCA Exam

Top 10 Most Useful PromQL Patterns
# ── RED METHOD (Rate, Errors, Duration) ──
Rate:     rate(http_requests_total{job="X"}[5m])
Errors:   rate(http_requests_total{job="X",status=~"5.."}[5m])
Duration: histogram_quantile(0.99,
            rate(http_duration_seconds_bucket{job="X"}[5m]))

# ── ERROR RATE PERCENTAGE ──
sum(rate(http_total{status=~"5.."}[5m])) / sum(rate(http_total[5m])) * 100

# ── UPTIME % (over last 30 days) ──
avg_over_time(up{job="X"}[30d]) * 100

# ── TOP 5 ENDPOINTS BY REQUEST RATE ──
topk(5, sum(rate(http_requests_total[5m])) by (endpoint))

# ── CPU USAGE % (per node) ──
100 - (avg by (instance) (rate(node_cpu_seconds_total{mode="idle"}[5m])) * 100)

# ── MEMORY USAGE % ──
(1 - (node_memory_MemAvailable_bytes / node_memory_MemTotal_bytes)) * 100

# ── DISK FILLING PREDICTION (4 hours) ──
predict_linear(node_filesystem_free_bytes{mountpoint="/"}[1h], 4*3600) < 0

# ── POD RESTART RATE ──
rate(kube_pod_container_status_restarts_total{namespace="anihpj"}[15m]) > 0

# ── SPIKE DETECTION (current > 2× 1h average) ──
rate(http_requests_total[5m])
> avg_over_time(rate(http_requests_total[5m])[1h]) * 2

# ── WEEK-OVER-WEEK COMPARISON ──
rate(http_requests_total[5m]) / rate(http_requests_total[5m] offset 1w)

# ── CPU SATURATION (per core) ──
rate(node_cpu_seconds_total{mode!="idle"}[5m])
  / on(instance) group_left
count by (instance)(node_cpu_seconds_total{mode="idle"})

# ── APDEX SCORE (API satisfaction) ──
# fast: < 300ms, tolerating: 300ms-1200ms, intolerable: > 1200ms
(sum(rate(http_duration_seconds_bucket{le="0.3"}[5m])) +
 sum(rate(http_duration_seconds_bucket{le="1.2"}[5m])) / 2)
  / sum(rate(http_duration_seconds_count[5m]))
⚠️ Top 5 PCA Exam PromQL Traps:
#1rate(metric) without [5m] — syntax error. Always: rate(metric[5m])
#2= vs =~ — exact match vs regex. Exam WILL test this distinction.
#3Applying rate() to a Gauge — returns garbage. rate() is ONLY for Counters.
#4histogram_quantile() on raw buckets — MUST rate() buckets first: histogram_quantile(0.99, rate(bucket[5m]))
#5sum by (le) when aggregating histograms — forgetting by(le) destroys bucket boundaries!

🌐 Prometheus HTTP API — Quick Reference

EndpointMethodPurpose
/api/v1/queryGETInstant query at a point in time. Params: query, time
/api/v1/query_rangeGETRange query over time window. Params: query, start, end, step
/api/v1/label/__name__/valuesGETList all metric names
/api/v1/label/{name}/valuesGETList all values for a label
/api/v1/seriesGETList time series matching selectors
/api/v1/targetsGETScrape target status (UP/DOWN)
/api/v1/rulesGETAlerting + recording rules
/api/v1/alertsGETActive alerts (firing + pending)
/api/v1/status/configGETCurrent Prometheus configuration
/api/v1/admin/tsdb/delete_seriesPOSTDelete time series (admin only)

Appendix B: prometheus.yml Reference (Annotated) REF

Every Field, Explained

A complete annotated prometheus.yml with all major configuration sections. Use this as your template for any project.

prometheus.yml — Complete Annotated Reference
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# GLOBAL CONFIGURATION
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
global:
  scrape_interval: 15s        # How often to scrape targets
  evaluation_interval: 15s    # How often to evaluate rules
  scrape_timeout: 10s         # Max time to wait for /metrics response
  external_labels:            # Labels added to ALL metrics from this Prom
    region: us-east-1
    prometheus: prom-a         # Identifies instance in HA setups

# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# RULE FILES — Alerting + Recording Rules
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
rule_files:
  - 'rules/anihpj_alerts.yml'      # Alerting rules
  - 'rules/anihpj_recording.yml'   # Recording rules

# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# ALERTING — Where to send firing alerts
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
alerting:
  alertmanagers:
    - static_configs:
        - targets: ['alertmanager:9093']
      timeout: 10s

# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# SCRAPE CONFIGURATIONS — What to monitor
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
scrape_configs:

  # ── Static targets (fixed IPs/DNS) ──
  - job_name: 'anihpj-django'
    scrape_interval: 15s        # Override global for this job
    metrics_path: '/metrics'    # Default, can be changed
    scheme: http                # http or https
    static_configs:
      - targets: ['web:8000']
        labels:
          env: production       # Add custom labels to these targets

  # ── File-based Service Discovery ──
  - job_name: 'file-sd'
    file_sd_configs:
      - files:
          - '/etc/prometheus/targets/*.json'
        refresh_interval: 5m    # Re-read files every 5 min

  # ── Kubernetes Service Discovery ──
  - job_name: 'kubernetes-pods'
    kubernetes_sd_configs:
      - role: pod               # Discover pods
        namespaces:
          names: ['anihpj']     # Only anihpj namespace
    relabel_configs:            # BEFORE scraping
      - source_labels: [__meta_kubernetes_pod_label_app]
        action: keep
        regex: anihpj-web        # Only pods with app=anihpj-web
      - source_labels: [__meta_kubernetes_namespace]
        target_label: namespace  # Map to regular label
    metric_relabel_configs:     # AFTER scraping (drop unwanted metrics)
      - source_labels: [__name__]
        regex: 'go_.*'          # Drop Go runtime metrics
        action: drop

  # ── Federation (pull from another Prometheus) ──
  - job_name: 'federate'
    honor_labels: true          # Keep original labels
    metrics_path: '/federate'
    params:
      'match[]':
        - '{job="anihpj-django"}'
        - '{__name__=~"job:.*"}'  # Only recording rules
    static_configs:
      - targets: ['prometheus-regional:9090']

# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# REMOTE WRITE — Send to long-term storage
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
remote_write:
  - url: "http://thanos-receive:19291/api/v1/receive"
    queue_config:
      capacity: 2500            # Buffer per shard
      max_shards: 200           # Max parallel senders
      max_samples_per_send: 500 # Batch size

# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# REMOTE READ — Query external storage
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
remote_read:
  - url: "http://thanos-query:10902/api/v1/read"
    read_recent: true           # Also query local TSDB

🔧 Relabel Configs Cookbook — Common Operations

TaskRelabel ConfigExplanation
Keep only matchingaction: keep; regex: anihpj-.*Drop all targets NOT matching regex
Drop matchingaction: drop; regex: test-.*Drop targets matching regex
Rename labeltarget_label: ns; replacement: $1; regex: (.*)Copy source label value to target_label
Replace label valuetarget_label: env; replacement: prodSet a fixed label value on all targets
Set __address__target_label: __address__; replacement: host:9187Change scrape target address
Hashmod (shard)action: hashmod; modulus: 3; target_label: shardSplit targets across N Prometheus shards
Labelmap (mass rename)action: labelmap; regex: __meta_(.*)Map all meta labels to regular labels

Appendix C: Alertmanager Configuration Reference REF

Complete alertmanager.yml — Annotated for Production

Every section of alertmanager.yml explained: global settings, route tree, receivers, and inhibition rules.

alertmanager.yml — Complete Annotated Production Config
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# GLOBAL — Default settings for all receivers
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
global:
  smtp_smarthost: 'smtp.anihpj.com:587'     # SMTP relay
  smtp_from: 'alertmanager@anihpj.com'      # From address
  smtp_auth_username: 'alertmanager'
  smtp_auth_password: 'supersecret'
  slack_api_url: 'https://hooks.slack.com/services/...'
  pagerduty_url: 'https://events.pagerduty.com/v2/enqueue'

# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# ROUTE — The route tree (root + children)
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
route:
  receiver: 'platform-email'         # Default (catch-all)
  group_by: ['alertname', 'namespace'] # Group by these labels
  group_wait: 30s                     # Wait for sibling alerts
  group_interval: 5m                  # Min time between group sends
  repeat_interval: 4h                 # Remind if still firing
  
  routes:                             # CHILD ROUTES
    # Critical → PagerDuty
    - match:
        severity: critical
      receiver: 'pagerduty-oncall'
      continue: true                  # Also try remaining routes
    
    # Warning → Slack
    - match_re:
        severity: 'warning|critical'  # Regex: warning OR critical
      receiver: 'slack-platform'
    
    # Database team → Own Slack channel
    - match:
        team: database
      receiver: 'slack-database-team'
    
    # Info → Email only
    - match:
        severity: info
      receiver: 'platform-email'

# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# INHIBIT RULES — Suppress B when A fires
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
inhibit_rules:
  - source_match:                     # When THIS fires...
      alertname: 'DatacenterDown'
    target_match_re:                  # Suppress THESE...
      alertname: '.*'                 # ALL alerts
    equal: ['datacenter']             # In the same datacenter

# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# RECEIVERS — Where alerts are delivered
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
receivers:
  # ── Email Receiver ──
  - name: 'platform-email'
    email_configs:
      - to: 'platform@anihpj.com'
        headers:
          Subject: '[{{ .Status | toUpper }}] {{ .GroupLabels.alertname }}'
  
  # ── PagerDuty Receiver ──
  - name: 'pagerduty-oncall'
    pagerduty_configs:
      - routing_key: 'abc123def456'
        severity: '{{ .CommonLabels.severity }}'
        description: '{{ .CommonAnnotations.description }}'
  
  # ── Slack Platform Team ──
  - name: 'slack-platform'
    slack_configs:
      - channel: '#platform-alerts'
        title: '{{ .GroupLabels.alertname }}'
        text: '{{ .CommonAnnotations.description }}'
        color: '{{ if eq .CommonLabels.severity "critical" }}danger{{ else }}warning{{ end }}'
  
  # ── Slack Database Team ──
  - name: 'slack-database-team'
    slack_configs:
      - channel: '#db-alerts'
        title: '[DB] {{ .GroupLabels.alertname }}'
  
  # ── Webhook (custom integration) ──
  - name: 'custom-webhook'
    webhook_configs:
      - url: 'https://api.anihpj.com/alerts'
        send_resolved: true            # Also notify when alert resolves

# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# TEMPLATES — Custom notification templates (optional)
# ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
templates:
  - '/etc/alertmanager/templates/*.tmpl'

🧭 Quick Decision: Which Receiver for Which Alert?

ScenarioReceiverConfig KeyBest For
🚨 Service is DOWN, users affectedPagerDutypagerduty_configsOn-call rotation, wake someone up
⚠️ Something needs attention soonSlack / Teamsslack_configsChat notification during business hours
📊 Info / audit trailEmailemail_configsReliable fallback, permanent record
🔗 Custom ticket/automationWebhookwebhook_configsAuto-create Jira tickets, trigger runbooks
📞 Alternative on-callOpsGenie / VictorOpsopsgenie_configsTeams already using these platforms

anihpj-api-dashboard.json — Import into Grafana
{
  "dashboard": {
    "title": "anihpj API Overview",
    "uid": "anihpj-red",
    "tags": ["anihpj", "django", "RED"],
    "timezone": "browser",

📊 Panel Reference — What Each Panel Shows

#PanelTypePromQLWhy This Matters
1Request Rate (RPS)timeseriessum(rate(http_requests_total{job="anihpj-django"}[5m])) by (endpoint)Shows traffic per endpoint. Spikes = deploy or attack. Drops = outage.
2Error Ratetimeseriessum(rate(http_requests_total{...,status=~"5.."}[5m]))Red line = user-facing errors. Flat at 0 is ideal.
3p99 Latencytimeserieshistogram_quantile(0.99, sum(rate(bucket[5m])) by (le, endpoint))99% of users experience this latency or less. If p99 climbs, something's slow.
4p50/p95/p99timeseries3 separate quantile queriesFull latency distribution: median (p50), tail (p95), worst-case (p99).
5Status Codesbargaugesum(rate(http_requests_total[5m])) by (status)Visual breakdown: 2xx (green), 4xx (yellow), 5xx (red).
6Active Listingsstatjobpost_active_listingsBusiness KPI — how many jobs are listed. Gauge from custom instrumentation.

Appendix E: Docker Compose — Full anihpj Monitoring Stack REF

One File, Complete Stack

The definitive docker-compose.yml for running anihpj Django with Prometheus, Grafana, Alertmanager, and all exporters. Also includes the Dockerfile, prometheus.yml, and alertmanager.yml that accompany it.

docker-compose.yml — anihpj + Full Monitoring Stack
version: '3.8'
services:
  # ── anihpj Django Application ──
  web:
    build: .
    ports: ["8000:8000"]
    environment:
      - DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE=anihpj.settings
      - DATABASE_URL=postgres://anihpj_user:supersecret@db:5432/anihpj
      - REDIS_URL=redis://redis:6379/0
    depends_on: [db, redis]
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "curl", "-f", "http://localhost:8000/metrics"]
      interval: 30s

  # ── PostgreSQL + Exporter ──
  db:
    image: postgres:15
    environment:
      - POSTGRES_DB=anihpj
      - POSTGRES_USER=anihpj_user
      - POSTGRES_PASSWORD=supersecret
    volumes: [pgdata:/var/lib/postgresql/data]

  pg-exporter:
    image: prometheuscommunity/postgres-exporter
    ports: ["9187:9187"]
    environment:
      - DATA_SOURCE_NAME=postgresql://anihpj_user:supersecret@db:5432/anihpj?sslmode=disable

  # ── Redis + Exporter ──
  redis:
    image: redis:7-alpine
    volumes: [redisdata:/data]

  redis-exporter:
    image: oliver006/redis_exporter
    ports: ["9121:9121"]
    command: ['--redis.addr=redis://redis:6379']

  # ── Prometheus ──
  prometheus:
    image: prom/prometheus:latest
    ports: ["9090:9090"]
    volumes:
      - ./prometheus.yml:/etc/prometheus/prometheus.yml
      - prometheus_data:/prometheus
    command:
      - '--config.file=/etc/prometheus/prometheus.yml'
      - '--storage.tsdb.path=/prometheus'
      - '--storage.tsdb.retention.time=15d'

  # ── Grafana ──
  grafana:
    image: grafana/grafana:latest
    ports: ["3000:3000"]
    environment:
      - GF_SECURITY_ADMIN_PASSWORD=admin
    volumes: [grafana_data:/var/lib/grafana]

  # ── Alertmanager ──
  alertmanager:
    image: prom/alertmanager:latest
    ports: ["9093:9093"]
    volumes:
      - ./alertmanager.yml:/etc/alertmanager/alertmanager.yml
    command:
      - '--config.file=/etc/alertmanager/alertmanager.yml'

  # ── Node Exporter ──
  node-exporter:
    image: prom/node-exporter:latest
    ports: ["9100:9100"]
    volumes:
      - /proc:/host/proc:ro
      - /sys:/host/sys:ro
      - /:/rootfs:ro
    command:
      - '--path.procfs=/host/proc'
      - '--path.sysfs=/host/sys'

volumes:
  pgdata:
  redisdata:
  prometheus_data:
  grafana_data:

# START: docker compose up -d
# ACCESS: Prometheus :9090 | Grafana :3000 (admin/admin) | Alertmanager :9093

ServicePortEndpointWhat Prometheus Scrapes
anihpj Django:8000/metricsdjango_http_*, jobpost_*
PostgreSQL Exporter:9187/metricspg_stat_*, pg_locks_*
Redis Exporter:9121/metricsredis_commands_*, redis_memory_*
Node Exporter:9100/metricsnode_cpu_*, node_memory_*, node_disk_*
Prometheus:9090/graph (UI), /api/v1 (API)Self-monitoring (prometheus_*)
Grafana:3000/ (UI)N/A (queries Prometheus, not scraped)

📁 File 2: jobpost/metrics.py — Custom Metric Definitions

metrics.py — All custom Prometheus metrics for jobpost
# jobpost/metrics.py
from prometheus_client import Counter, Gauge, Histogram

# COUNTERS — Track cumulative events
jobpost_jobs_created_total = Counter('jobpost_jobs_created_total', 'Job postings created', ['status'])
jobpost_applications_total = Counter('jobpost_applications_total', 'Applications submitted', ['status', 'source'])
jobpost_errors_total = Counter('jobpost_errors_total', 'Application errors', ['error_type'])

# GAUGES — Track current state
jobpost_active_listings = Gauge('jobpost_active_listings', 'Currently active jobs')
jobpost_queue_depth = Gauge('jobpost_queue_depth', 'Pending background tasks', ['queue_name'])
jobpost_skills_per_job = Gauge('jobpost_skills_per_job', 'Average skills per posting')

# HISTOGRAMS — Track distributions
jobpost_request_duration_seconds = Histogram(
    'jobpost_request_duration_seconds', 'Request latency',
    ['view', 'method'],
    buckets=[0.01, 0.025, 0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.5, 5.0, 10.0]
)
jobpost_search_duration_seconds = Histogram(
    'jobpost_search_duration_seconds', 'Search latency',
    buckets=[0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.5, 5.0]
)

📁 File 3: Dockerfile — Production-Ready Instrumented Image

Dockerfile — anihpj with Prometheus instrumentation
FROM python:3.11-slim
ENV PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1
WORKDIR /app
RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends gcc libpq-dev curl && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*
COPY requirements.txt .
RUN pip install --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt
COPY . .
RUN python manage.py collectstatic --noinput
EXPOSE 8000
HEALTHCHECK --interval=30s --timeout=3s --start-period=5s --retries=3 CMD curl -f http://localhost:8000/metrics || exit 1
CMD ["gunicorn", "anihpj.wsgi:application", "--bind", "0.0.0.0:8000", "--workers", "4"]

📁 File 4: jobpost/views.py — Instrumented Views

views.py — Business logic with inline Prometheus instrumentation
# jobpost/views.py — Key view functions with metrics
import time
from django.shortcuts import render, get_object_or_404
from django.http import JsonResponse
from .models import JobPost, Application
from .metrics import (
    jobpost_jobs_created_total, jobpost_applications_total,
    jobpost_active_listings, jobpost_request_duration_seconds,
    jobpost_search_duration_seconds, jobpost_skills_per_job,
)

def job_list(request):
    start = time.time()
    jobs = JobPost.objects.filter(status='active')
    
    # Update gauge: current active count
    jobpost_active_listings.set(JobPost.objects.filter(status='active').count())
    
    # Track search latency if query present
    if request.GET.get('q'):
        jobpost_search_duration_seconds.observe(time.time() - start)
    
    jobpost_request_duration_seconds.labels(view='job_list', method='GET').observe(time.time() - start)
    return render(request, 'jobpost/job_list.html', {'jobs': jobs})

def job_detail(request, job_id):
    start = time.time()
    job = get_object_or_404(JobPost, pk=job_id)
    jobpost_request_duration_seconds.labels(view='job_detail', method='GET').observe(time.time() - start)
    return render(request, 'jobpost/job_detail.html', {'job': job})

def job_apply(request, job_id):
    job = get_object_or_404(JobPost, pk=job_id)
    if request.method == 'POST':
        try:
            Application.objects.create(job=job, applicant=request.user)
            jobpost_applications_total.labels(status='submitted', source='web').inc()
            return JsonResponse({'status': 'success'})
        except Exception:
            jobpost_applications_total.labels(status='error', source='web').inc()
            return JsonResponse({'status': 'error'}, status=500)
    return render(request, 'jobpost/job_apply.html', {'job': job})
💡 Instrumentation Pattern: ① Define metrics at module level in metrics.py. ② Import into views. ③ Call .inc()/.observe()/.set() at the right moment. ④ django-prometheus middleware handles HTTP+DB+Cache metrics automatically. ⑤ The /metrics endpoint is auto-created by django_prometheus.urls.

📁 File 5: anihpj/urls.py — Wire Up /metrics

urls.py — Expose the Prometheus metrics endpoint
# anihpj/urls.py
from django.urls import path, include
from django.contrib import admin

urlpatterns = [
    path('', include('django_prometheus.urls')),  # ← /metrics lives here!
    path('admin/', admin.site.urls),
    path('jobs/', include('jobpost.urls')),
]
# Verify: curl http://localhost:8000/metrics | grep django_http

1Install packagesrequirements.txtprometheus-client + django-prometheus

📊 Domain 1: Observability Concepts (18%) — Questions 1-11

Q1

What are the three pillars of observability?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Metrics, Logs, Traces. Metrics = numerical data (Prometheus), Logs = text records (Loki), Traces = request journeys (Tempo/Jaeger).

Q2

Does Prometheus use a Push or Pull model for data collection?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Pull. Prometheus scrapes /metrics endpoints. PushGateway is a special case for batch jobs, not the default.

Q3

What does SLO stand for and how does it differ from SLA?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

SLO = Service Level Objective (internal target, e.g., 99.9% uptime). SLA = Service Level Agreement (contract with customer, includes penalties). SLOs drive monitoring; SLAs drive business.

Q4

What is an SLI?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Service Level Indicator — the actual measured value. Example: "Our SLI (measured error rate) is 0.02%, which is below our SLO of 0.1%."

Q5

Which Prometheus component handles alert routing and deduplication?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Alertmanager. Prometheus fires alerts; Alertmanager deduplicates, groups, routes, and silences them.

Q6

What is a time series in Prometheus?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

A metric name + set of labels + sequence of timestamp-value pairs. Each unique label combination is a separate time series.

Q7

How does tracing complement Prometheus metrics?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Tracing shows a single request's journey through microservices (spans). Metrics show aggregate behavior. Together: "p99 latency is high" (metric) → "which service in the chain is slow?" (trace).

Q8

What is the difference between Blackbox and Whitebox monitoring?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Blackbox: testing from outside (Blackbox Exporter probes URLs). Whitebox: internal metrics from the app itself (/metrics endpoint). Prometheus does both.

Q9

What is the purpose of the PushGateway?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

For batch/cron jobs that end before Prometheus can scrape them. The job pushes metrics to PushGateway; Prometheus scrapes PushGateway. NOT for long-running services.

Q10

What is the Golden Signals methodology?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Latency, Traffic, Errors, Saturation. Google SRE's 4 essential signals for monitoring any service. Covers both RED (Latency/Traffic/Errors) and USE (Saturation).

Q11

What format does Prometheus use to expose metrics?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Text-based exposition format with HELP, TYPE, and metric lines. Example: http_requests_total{method="GET"} 1234 1609746000. OpenMetrics is the evolving standard.

📊 Domain 2: Prometheus Fundamentals (20%) — Questions 12-23

Q12

What are the four main sections of prometheus.yml?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

global, scrape_configs, rule_files, alerting. global = defaults. scrape_configs = what to monitor. rule_files = recording + alerting rules. alerting = where to send alerts.

Q13

What is the default scrape_interval?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

15 seconds (configured in global.scrape_interval). Can be overridden per job.

Q14

What two labels does Prometheus automatically add to every metric?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

job (scrape job name) and instance (host:port of the scraped target). Added during scraping, not by the application.

Q15

What is the difference between relabel_configs and metric_relabel_configs?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

relabel_configs apply BEFORE scraping (filter targets, modify labels). metric_relabel_configs apply AFTER scraping (drop/rewrite metric labels on ingested data).

Q16

How does Prometheus store data on disk?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

In the TSDB (Time Series Database) as 2-hour blocks. Default retention is 15 days. Each block contains chunks of compressed time-series data.

Q17

What are the four metric types in Prometheus?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Counter (only increases), Gauge (up/down), Histogram (bucketed observations), Summary (client-side quantiles).

Q18

What happens if two time series have the same metric name but different label values?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

They are different time series. Prometheus treats them independently. Each unique label combination = one time series.

Q19

What is staleness in Prometheus?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

When a target stops being scraped, its metrics are marked stale after 5 minutes and disappear from query results. This is why monitoring UP/DOWN targets is important.

Q20

What is the __name__ label?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

The internal label that stores the metric name. http_requests_total actually has label __name__="http_requests_total". Used in relabeling to filter by metric name.

Q21

What is the evaluation_interval?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

How often Prometheus evaluates alerting and recording rules. Default: 1 minute. Separate from scrape_interval.

Q22

What does honor_labels do in a scrape config?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

If true, keeps labels from the scraped target even if they conflict with Prometheus-added labels. If false (default), Prometheus labels override target labels. Critical for federation.

Q23

What is the purpose of external_labels?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Labels added to ALL metrics from this Prometheus instance. Used in HA setups to identify which instance scraped the data, and in federation to track data origin.

📊 Domain 3: PromQL (28%) — Questions 24-40

Q24

What's the difference between an instant vector and a range vector?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Instant vector: one value per series (http_requests_total). Range vector: multiple values over [duration] (http_requests_total[5m]). Range vectors are input to rate(), increase(), etc.

Q25

What does rate(http_requests_total[5m]) calculate?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Per-second average rate of http_requests_total over the last 5 minutes. The [5m] is MANDATORY — rate() requires a range vector.

Q26

How does irate() differ from rate()?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

irate() uses only the last TWO samples (instant rate, spikier). rate() averages over ALL samples in the range (smoother). Use rate() for alerting, irate() for graphs.

Q27

What does increase(http_requests_total[1h]) return?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

The total increase of the counter over the last hour. Extrapolated to cover the full range. Equivalent to rate(x[1h]) * 3600 but more accurate for integer counts.

Q28

What's the difference between = and =~ in PromQL?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

= is exact match. =~ is regex match. Example: status=~"5.." matches 500, 501, 502, etc. NOT ==~ — that's a syntax error!

Q29

What does sum by (endpoint) do?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Aggregates time series by summing, keeping the endpoint label. sum by (endpoint) (rate(requests[5m])) = one result per endpoint. Use without(endpoint) to drop endpoint instead.

Q30

Why must you rate() histogram buckets before histogram_quantile()?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Buckets are counters — they only increase. histogram_quantile() needs a rate of increase to calculate meaningful percentiles. Raw counter values represent accumulated counts, not current distribution.

Q31

What does the le label mean in histogram buckets?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

"Less than or Equal to" — the bucket boundary. le="0.5" counts observations ≤ 0.5 seconds. le="+Inf" is the catch-all bucket (counts everything).

Q32

What is the difference between sum by() and sum without()?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

by(X) = keep label X, aggregate everything else. without(X) = drop label X, keep everything else. sum by (endpoint) = one result per endpoint. sum without (instance) = combine all instances.

Q33

What does topk(5, metric) do?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Returns the top 5 time series with the highest values. topk(5, sum(rate(requests[5m])) by (endpoint)) = busiest 5 endpoints.

Q34

What does the offset modifier do?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Shifts the query back in time. rate(requests[5m] offset 1w) = "what was the rate at this exact time, 1 week ago?" Used for week-over-week comparisons.

Q35

What is the @ modifier?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Evaluates the query at a specific Unix timestamp. http_requests_total @ 1609746000 = "what was the value at this exact second?" Used for debugging historical data.

Q36

What does absent(metric) return?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Returns 1 if the metric has NO data, empty if it exists. Critical for alerting: "is this metric completely missing?" Used to detect if a target stopped reporting entirely.

Q37

What does predict_linear() do?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Predicts a value t seconds in the future based on linear regression of the last [d] of data. predict_linear(disk_free[1h], 4*3600) < 0 = "will disk be full in 4 hours?"

Q38

What is vector matching with on() and group_left?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Used when dividing/multiplying metrics with different label sets. rate(cpu[5m]) / on(instance) group_left count by (instance) (cpu_cores) = CPU per-core usage. on() specifies match labels, group_left adds labels from the right side.

Q39

What does avg_over_time(metric[1h]) do?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Calculates the average of all values in the range. Input: range vector → Output: instant vector (one value per series). Unlike rate(), this works on gauges too.

Q40

Which function would you use to detect a counter reset?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

resets(). Returns the number of counter resets within the range. Useful for detecting application restarts. rate() and increase() automatically handle resets correctly.

📊 Domain 4: Instrumentation & Exporters (16%) — Questions 41-50

Q41

What Python library is used to add Prometheus metrics to Django?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

django-prometheus (wraps prometheus_client). Provides middleware, DB backend, cache backend. Just add to INSTALLED_APPS and MIDDLEWARE.

Q42

Which metric type should you use for counting HTTP requests?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Counter. Requests only increase. Use .inc() in code, rate() in PromQL. Name with _total suffix: http_requests_total.

Q43

What port does the Node Exporter use?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

:9100. Deployed as a DaemonSet on Kubernetes. Exposes CPU, memory, disk, network metrics.

Q44

Why is Histogram preferred over Summary for distributed systems?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Histogram buckets can be aggregated across instances (sum the _bucket series). Summary quantiles are calculated on the client and cannot be combined. Choose Histogram.

Q45

Which of these should NOT be a label: endpoint, method, user_id, status_code?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

user_id. Unbounded cardinality. Each user creates a new time series. Endpoint (~50), method (~5), status_code (~20) are all safe low-cardinality labels.

Q46

What does django-prometheus give you automatically?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

HTTP request counts by view, latency histograms, response counts by status, database query duration, DB errors, cache hit/miss rates — all with zero custom code.

Q47

What is an exporter in Prometheus?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

A translator that converts app-specific metrics to Prometheus format at /metrics. Runs as a separate process/sidecar. No code changes to the monitored application.

Q48

What is the naming convention for Prometheus metrics?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

namespace_subsystem_name_unit — lowercase with underscores. Counters end in _total. Example: jobpost_http_requests_total. Units: _seconds, _bytes, _ratio.

Q49

What is the RED method?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Rate (requests/sec), Errors (failed/sec), Duration (latency). The 3 metrics every service endpoint should expose. Rate and Errors are Counters; Duration is a Histogram.

Q50

What OpenTelemetry feature instruments Django without code changes?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Auto-instrumentation. opentelemetry-instrument python manage.py runserver monkey-patches Django to emit metrics. Less control but instant setup.

📊 Domain 5: Alerting & Dashboards (18%) — Questions 51-60

Q51

What are the 5 fields of a Prometheus alerting rule?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

alert, expr, for, labels, annotations. alert and expr are required. for prevents flapping. labels are for routing; annotations are for human readers.

Q52

What does the for field do in an alert rule?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Prevents flapping. The alert only fires if the condition is true for the ENTIRE for duration. Without it, a 1-second spike triggers the alert. Typical: 5m for errors, 2m for DB down.

Q53

What are the 5 Alertmanager functions in order?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Deduplication → Grouping → Routing → Inhibition → Silencing. Memorize this order — guaranteed exam question.

Q54

What is the difference between recording rules and alerting rules?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Recording rules (record:) pre-compute expensive queries into new metrics. Alerting rules (alert:) fire notifications when conditions are true. Recording rules have no for, no annotations.

Q55

What is Grafana's role with Prometheus?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Visualization only. Grafana queries Prometheus (data source), renders graphs/dashboards. It does NOT store metrics or scrape targets. Always needs Prometheus behind it.

Q56

What are Grafana dashboard variables used for?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Dynamic, reusable dashboards. Variables like $namespace create dropdowns. One dashboard for all environments — select "dev" or "prod" and all panels update.

Q57

What does group_by do in Alertmanager?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

Groups alerts with same label values into ONE notification. group_by: ['alertname'] = 50 pod crashes → one message, not 50 pages.

Q58

What is inhibition in Alertmanager?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

When a higher-priority alert fires, suppress lower-priority ones. Example: datacenter is down → don't also page about individual app errors.

Q59

What is the naming convention for recording rule output metrics?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

level:metric:operation — colon-separated. level (job/instance), metric (what's measured), operation (what was done). Example: job:http_errors:rate5m.

Q60

Why should silences always have an expiry?

Click to reveal answer
✅ A

People forget. A silence without expiry mutes alerts forever — you'll only discover it after an outage. Always set endsAt or a duration. Use maintenance windows with clear end times.

Passing: 45/60 (75%). Domain weights: Observability=11 questions, Fundamentals=12, PromQL=17, Instrumentation=10, Alerting=10. If you score ≥80% here, you're ready for the real exam. Time yourself: 2 minutes per question max. If stuck, mark and return.

Appendix H: Common PromQL Patterns & Recipes REF

Advanced PromQL for Production Monitoring

Error budgets, SLO tracking, anomaly detection, forecasting — the patterns that take you from basic monitoring to production excellence.

💰 Error Budget & SLO Patterns

Error Budget & SLO Tracking PromQL
# ERROR BUDGET REMAINING (30-day window, 99.9% SLO)
(1 - 0.999) * 30 * 24 * 3600 = 2592 seconds budget

# ERROR BUDGET BURN RATE (is budget burning too fast?)
rate(http_errors_total[1h]) / (rate(http_total[1h]) * 0.001)
# > 1 = burning budget faster than allowed

# SLO COMPLIANCE (last 30 days — should be > 99.9%)
sum(rate(http_total{status!~"5.."}[30d])) / sum(rate(http_total[30d]))

# APDEX SCORE (API satisfaction: fast

🔍 Anomaly Detection & Forecasting

Detecting Problems Before Users Notice
# WEEK-OVER-WEEK COMPARISON (traffic anomaly)
rate(http_total[5m]) / rate(http_total[5m] offset 1w)
# > 1.5 = 50%+ increase vs same time last week

# SPIKE DETECTION (current > 2× the 1-hour average)
rate(http_requests_total[5m])
> avg_over_time(rate(http_requests_total[5m])[1h]) * 2

# DISK FULL PREDICTION (will disk be full in 24h?)
predict_linear(node_filesystem_free_bytes{mountpoint="/"}[1h], 24 * 3600) < 0

# MEMORY LEAK DETECTION (steady decrease over 6h)
deriv(node_memory_MemAvailable_bytes[6h]) < 0
# Negative derivative = memory shrinking = potential leak

# SLOW QUERY DETECTION (p99 DB query time > 500ms)
histogram_quantile(0.99, rate(django_db_query_duration_seconds_bucket[5m])) > 0.5

# CACHE EFFICIENCY (hit rate below 80% = investigate)
rate(django_cache_hits_total[5m]) /
  (rate(django_cache_hits_total[5m]) + rate(django_cache_misses_total[5m])) < 0.8

🧭 When to Use Each Pattern — Quick Decision Guide

You Want to Know...Use This PatternChapter
Is my service meeting its SLO?SLO Compliance queryCh 1
How fast are we burning error budget?Error Budget Burn RateCh 1
Are users satisfied with API speed?Apdex ScoreCh 1
Is traffic abnormally high right now?Spike Detection (2× average)Ch 8
Is this week's traffic unusual?Week-over-Week comparisonCh 8
Will disk fill up soon?predict_linear() forecastCh 5,11
Is there a memory leak?deriv() trend detectionCh 5
Are DB queries getting slower?p99 DB query timeCh 8,10
Is the cache effective?Cache Hit RateCh 10
PCA Exam: Which PromQL Patterns Are Tested?

The PCA exam focuses on PRACTICAL PromQL. The patterns most likely to appear: (1) rate() on counters — you WILL be asked to write or interpret a rate query. (2) histogram_quantile() — know the full pipeline: bucket → rate() → sum by(le) → histogram_quantile(). (3) predict_linear() for disk-filling alerts — exam favorite because it tests both function knowledge and alert logic. (4) sum by() vs sum without() — the exam loves testing aggregation dimension control. (5) offset for comparisons — "compare this week to last week" is a classic question pattern.

Appendix I: Glossary — Prometheus & Observability Terms REF

A-Z Reference with Chapter Cross-References

Every technical term used in this guide, defined concisely with links to the relevant chapter.

TermDefinitionCh
Alerting RuleYAML-defined condition (PromQL expr) that fires an alert when true. Sent to Alertmanager.11
AlertmanagerService that receives alerts from Prometheus. Deduplicates, groups, routes, inhibits, silences.12
Agent ModeLightweight Prometheus (--agent) that only scrapes + remote-writes. No local storage or queries.15
CardinalityNumber of unique label value combinations. High cardinality causes memory/performance issues.2
cAdvisorContainer Advisor: built into kubelet, exposes container CPU/memory/disk/network metrics at :10250.16
CounterMetric type that only increases (or resets to 0). Use rate() on counters. Example: http_requests_total.2,9
DaemonSetKubernetes resource that runs one pod per node. Used for Node Exporter deployment.5
Exposition FormatText format at /metrics: HELP line, TYPE line, metric_name{labels} value [timestamp].2
ExportersPrograms that expose third-party metrics in Prometheus format at /metrics. Run as sidecars.5
FederationHierarchical Prometheus: regional instances scrape targets, global scrapes /federate from regionals.15
GaugeMetric type that goes up and down. Use directly (no rate needed). Example: memory_usage_bytes.2,9
GrafanaVisualization platform. Queries Prometheus (and 100+ sources) to create dashboards. Not a metrics store.14
HistogramMetric type with configurable buckets. Creates _bucket, _sum, _count series. Use histogram_quantile().2,9
Instant VectorSet of time series, each with ONE sample. Result of most PromQL expressions. Query: http_requests_total.6
JobAuto-label from prometheus.yml scrape job name. Used for grouping targets. Always present on metrics.4
kube-state-metricsService that exposes K8s object STATE (deployment replicas, pod phase, node conditions). Not resource usage.16
LabelsKey-value pairs on metrics for dimensional filtering. Keep cardinality low. Example: {method="GET"}.2
PodMonitorPrometheus Operator CRD that scrapes pods directly by label (no Service needed). Alternative to ServiceMonitor.10,16
PromQLPrometheus Query Language. Functional language for selecting, aggregating, and transforming time series.6-8
Range VectorSet of time series, each with MULTIPLE samples over [duration]. Input to rate(), increase(), etc.6
Recording RulePre-computes expensive PromQL and stores result as new metric. Uses record: field. Dashboard speed boost.13
RED MethodRate (requests/sec), Errors (failed/sec), Duration (latency). The 3 metrics every service endpoint needs.9
RelabelingModifying labels before (relabel_configs) or after (metric_relabel_configs) scraping. Filtering + transforming.4
Remote WriteSends scraped metrics to external storage (Thanos/Cortex/VictoriaMetrics) for long-term retention.15
ScrapePrometheus HTTP GET to /metrics endpoint. Happens every scrape_interval (default 15s). Pull model.3
Service DiscoveryAutomatic target detection. Types: static, file, kubernetes, consul, ec2, dns, etc. Critical on K8s.4
ServiceMonitorPrometheus Operator CRD. Defines scrape targets by matching Service labels. Preferred on K8s.10,16
SilenceAlertmanager feature to mute alerts during maintenance. Always set an expiry!12
SummaryMetric type with client-side quantiles. CANNOT be aggregated across instances. Prefer Histogram.9
TSDBTime Series Database. Prometheus stores data in 2-hour blocks. Default retention: 15 days.3
USE MethodUtilization, Saturation, Errors. For monitoring RESOURCES (CPU, memory, disk). Complement to RED.9
